Like, it's pitch black outside, maybe 4 AM, and I just lie there staring at the ceiling feeling like I never even rested at all. It's so incredibly frustrating because I know I need the sleep, and I just lie there trying to force it, but my brain just won't cooperate. I've tried turning off my phone early, I've tried tea, but nothing is working and it's just... it's exhausting, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different things, even when there isn’t an immediate problem to solve?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't really call it worrying, exactly. I mean, sure, I think about work, like I mentioned, because if I mess up a spreadsheet or an account reconciliation, it's a huge deal for my boss. But it's not like I'm lying there agonizing over... I don't know, my health, or the economy, or what someone said to me three weeks ago. It's not this constant dread or panic about things going wrong. \n\nIt's more just... my brain is just on. It's like a TV left on in the background that you can't find the remote for. And it's not always negative or anxious worry, either. Sometimes I'm just thinking about a movie I watched, or I'm planning out how I'm going to reorganize my apartment, or just completely random stuff. Like the other night, I think it was Tuesday, I literally spent two hours in the dark mentally redesigning a kitchen in a house I don't even own—I rent my place, so I can't even paint the cabinets if I wanted to! \n\nSo, yeah, it's not really worrying about problems that don't exist. People always ask me if I'm just stressed out, and I always have to explain that it's not really stress, it's just... noise. Lots and lots of noise and thoughts going a mile a minute, but not necessarily worrying. I just can't get it to power down so I can actually sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I really have. Honestly, it's been pretty bad. It's hard to even put into words because it's not just feeling a little sad about something specific, it's this... this incredibly heavy, empty feeling. Lately, I'll wake up—or, you know, when I'm just lying awake in the early morning like I was saying before—and that weight just hits me right in the chest before I even get out of bed. \n\nAnd the thing that really gets me, the thing that makes it so confusing and frustrating, is that I'll just be sitting there in my own condo, and I worked so, so hard for this place. I mean, I only have my high school diploma, so working my way up in bookkeeping and saving every single extra penny to finally get my own place was a massive deal for me. It took years of being super careful with my money. I should be so proud of it. I should be looking around at my space and feeling great about what I've accomplished. \n\nBut instead, I just sit there and I just want to cry. I'll literally just be sitting on my couch in this place I busted my tail to buy, and tears will just start welling up, and everything just feels so completely hollow and pointless. It doesn't make any sense, but I just can't shake that heavy sadness. It just hangs over me, making the whole day feel like I'm walking through wet cement, even when I'm just at home where I should be happy.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I really have. And honestly, that's one of the things that bothers me the most right now, because I've always been someone who had hobbies, you know? Like, to balance out the boring spreadsheet stuff at work, I always tried to make sure I was doing fun things for myself. But lately... I don't know. \n\nTake my salsa class, for example. I used to go every single Thursday night. I even bought these specific shoes for it, and I loved the music and just getting to move around after sitting at a desk all week. But I just... I completely stopped going. I think I missed one week, and then another, and now the thought of actually getting dressed and going down to the dance studio just feels entirely pointless. I can't muster up a single ounce of care for it, which is so unlike me. \n\nAnd it's not just going out, it's stuff at home, too. I've always really liked painting. Just watercolors, nothing professional, but it was my way to unwind in the evenings. I actually have my paints and my brushes and everything still sitting out on my dining table right now. I left them out weeks ago thinking, 'Oh, I'll definitely paint this weekend, I just need to leave it out so I remember.' But they've just been sitting there gathering dust. \n\nI walk past that table every single day, and I look at my paints, and I just feel... nothing. Absolutely nothing. It's the weirdest, saddest sensation. It's honestly like they belong to somebody else. Like I'm looking at a stranger's hobbies in a stranger's apartment. I used to lose hours just mixing colors and now I can't even bring myself to pick up a brush, and I just don't understand where the girl who loved doing those things went. Everything just feels so incredibly flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: No... no, I haven't. I mean, I know I just said everything feels completely pointless, and it does, it really does feel that way. But actually wanting to hurt myself? No, I would never do that. It's more like... I just want to pause everything, you know? \n\nI was actually thinking about this just the other day when I was driving to the grocery store. I was sitting at this red light on 4th street—it's this notoriously long light, you can sit there for like three whole minutes—and I was just feeling so incredibly heavy, like I was telling you before. And I just stared at the steering wheel and I realized that I don't want to die, I just really, really don't want to have to deal with today. Or tomorrow, for that matter. \n\nI would never actually take steps to end my life or anything like that. For one thing, I think about my family, and even just my cat, Barnaby. Like, who would feed Barnaby? He has a very specific wet food diet because of his stomach issues, and I couldn't just leave him. And like I said about my condo, I worked way too hard balancing books and saving every single penny to just throw it all away. I don't want to lose what I've built, even if I can't seem to enjoy it right now. \n\nSo, no, the thought is never that I want to end it all. It's more just this desperate wish that I could go to sleep and wake up when this whole heavy, empty phase is just over. Like hibernation, almost! If I could just hibernate like a bear for a month or two until I feel like myself again, that would be absolutely perfect. But no, no actual thoughts of hurting myself. I definitely couldn't do that.\n\nDoctor: For about how long have you been feeling this depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Well... I'd say it's been a while now. This whole heavy, depressed, guilty fog... it's been hanging around for about five months, I think. Yeah, right around five months. It's funny, because it really started creeping in back in the late winter, just when everything was supposed to be coming together. \n\nI mean, I remember the exact week because it was right when I was finalizing the closing on the condo. I had all these meetings with the mortgage broker, and I was signing what felt like a million pieces of paper. And you'd think I'd be ecstatic, right? Like, I'm 26, I only have a high school diploma, and I'm buying property. I should have been over the moon. But instead, I just started feeling this... this creeping sense of dread and sadness. \n\nAnd then, after I actually bought the place and moved in, it just got so much worse. The first couple of weeks were okay, I guess, just because I was so busy unpacking boxes and figuring out where to put my couch and everything. But once the dust settled and it was just me and Barnaby in this new place... the bottom just completely fell out. That's when the really deep emptiness started. So, it's been about five months total of feeling like this, but definitely much, much worse over the last couple of months since the move. It's just so frustrating because this was supposed to be my big, happy milestone, you know? And instead, it's just been this endless, dragging weight for almost half a year now.\n\nDoctor: Did your sleep problems start before you began feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, no, actually, the sleep stuff is pretty much what finally made me make an appointment. I mean, the depression has been there for five months, like I said, but the sleep problems... they really started acting up more recently, and that's honestly what brings me in today. It's just gotten so incredibly messed up, and I can't function like this anymore. \n\nIt's like... it's like my brain forgot how to sleep properly. Half the time, I'll go to bed, you know, do my whole routine—wash my face, brush my teeth, make sure Barnaby has his water—and I'll get under the covers, and my mind just starts running. And I don't even know what it's running about! It's just racing, and I'll end up staring at the ceiling, staying up half the night just tossing and turning. I'll look at the clock and it's 2 AM, then 3 AM, and I'm just lying there. \n\nAnd then, if I do manage to fall asleep, or on the nights where I don't have the racing thoughts at the beginning, I end up waking up way, way too early. Like, I'll jolt awake at 4:30 in the morning, and the alarm isn't set until 7:00, and I just feel completely exhausted. Like I never even rested at all. My body feels heavy, my eyes burn, but I can't get back to sleep. So I just lie there in the dark, in this new condo that I'm supposed to be so happy about, just feeling miserable and exhausted. It's so frustrating because I'm an accountant, well, a bookkeeping clerk, and I need my brain to be sharp for the spreadsheets and the numbers, but I'm just running on fumes. So yeah, the sleep getting this bad is really the main reason I'm sitting here talking to you right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking noticeably more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Well, it's funny you ask that, because I've actually noticed both, and it's driving me completely crazy. Like, at work... I'm a bookkeeping clerk, right? So my whole day is numbers and spreadsheets and calculators. And lately, there are times when I'm just sitting there at my desk, and I literally catch myself moving in slow motion. Like, I'll be holding a stack of invoices, and I'll just stare at the calculator for what feels like forever before I even press the first button. It's like my brain has to send a special, certified letter to my hand just to get it to move. My boss actually asked me the other day if I was feeling okay because it took me twice as long to finish the end-of-day reconciliation. I just felt so heavy and sluggish, like I was moving through molasses. \n\nBut then... then there are other times where it's the exact opposite! It's so weird. Like, I'll be sitting there, and suddenly I just can't sit still. My leg will start bouncing under the desk, and I'll be fidgeting with all the little loose receipts on my desk, folding them up into tiny squares or rolling them into little tubes. I'll pick up a pen and click it a hundred times until my coworker gives me a look, and then I'll realize I'm doing it and try to stop, but I just feel this... this restless energy, like I have to be moving something. \n\nSo it's like I'm either a complete zombie who can barely lift a finger to type in a number, or I'm this jittery mess who can't stop playing with the office supplies. There's really no in-between right now. And it's so frustrating because I used to be so efficient! I could fly through a stack of expense reports in no time, and now it's just this constant battle of either forcing myself to move or trying to force myself to sit still. It's exhausting, honestly. Just another thing that's completely out of whack lately.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you ever felt you needed much less sleep than usual and still had plenty of energy the next day?\n\nPatient: Oh, yeah... actually, yes. It's so strange to think about now because of how completely drained I feel lately, but before I crashed into this awful slump, there was this stretch of time where that exact thing happened. It was right around when I was doing all the initial condo paperwork, I think. \n\nI just remember there were several nights where I would only sleep maybe three or four hours, tops. I'd be up until 2 or 3 in the morning because my brain was just buzzing with ideas—I was looking up living room furniture online, re-doing all my personal budget spreadsheets to account for the new mortgage, organizing literally everything. And then I'd wake up at like 6 AM, and the crazy part is, I wasn't tired at all. I got up feeling completely charged. \n\nUsually, I am the kind of person who needs a massive travel mug of dark roast just to function and look at the numbers at work, but during that time? It was like coffee wasn't even necessary. I would just bounce right out of bed, take Barnaby for a long walk, maybe even do a load of laundry or scrub the baseboards before I even had to leave for the office. I honestly thought I was just finally hitting my stride, you know? Like, I thought, \"Wow, this is what it feels like to be a successful, motivated adult. I'm 26, I'm buying a place, I'm crushing it at the bookkeeping firm.\" I was flying through expense reports so fast my boss was actually impressed. I just felt like I had this unlimited battery pack. But then, of course, it just completely vanished, and now I'm stuck feeling like a zombie who can't sleep properly anyway. But yeah, for a little while there, three or four hours was all I needed and I felt completely unstoppable.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you only slept a few hours and still felt energized, did you also feel unusually upbeat or unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, it was a really bizarre mix of both, which I know sounds totally contradictory, but that's exactly how it was. Like, at first, and for a lot of that time, I felt incredible. Like I was just... lit up from the inside. I felt so upbeat and positive, like everything was finally falling into place. I was getting the condo, I was organizing my life, I felt completely unstoppable. I remember walking into the office and just beaming at everyone, feeling like I could take on the world and balance every single ledger in the building before lunch. It was this amazing high, like I was the best version of myself. \n\nBut then... the weird part is, it could just flip on a dime. Like, out of nowhere, I would get so incredibly irritable. And not just a little annoyed, but like, skin-crawlingly frustrated over the absolute smallest things. I remember one day my mom called me to ask a super basic question about the closing costs for the condo—something she was just trying to be helpful about, you know?—and I completely snapped at her. I practically bit her head off on the phone, telling her she was hovering and treating me like a child, which was so unfair of me. And it wasn't just her. At work, my supervisor came over to ask a really standard question about how I coded one of the vendor invoices—something he does all the time, totally normal—and I felt this rush of pure anger. I had to physically bite the inside of my cheek to stop myself from yelling at him that he was slowing me down. I just gave him this incredibly short, snippy answer and glared at him until he walked away. \n\nNormally, I am not like that at all! I'm usually the one who brings donuts on Fridays and helps people find the typos in their spreadsheets. I don't snap at people. But during those few days, it was like I was this unstoppable force, and if anyone dared to interrupt my flow or ask a question, I just couldn't handle it. It was like they were moving in slow motion and I was moving at light speed, and their slowness was just infuriating to me. So yeah, I was super upbeat, but also ready to bite someone's head off if they looked at me wrong. It was exhausting for everyone around me, I'm sure.\n\nDoctor: About how many days in a row did that energized period last?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... how many days in a row? You know, I've actually tried to figure that out myself, looking back, but... well, it's incredibly hard to pin down an exact number. Because when you're only sleeping three or four hours a night, the days just sort of... they bleed into one another, you know? It's like one long, continuous stretch of daytime. You don't get that normal reset of a full eight hours, so Tuesday just rolls right into Wednesday, and suddenly it's Friday and you're still running on this bizarre high. \n\nI just remember it was right in the thick of all that condo stuff. I'd be up at 2 AM sending emails to my realtor—who probably thought I was completely insane, by the way, getting emails at that hour about escrow and title insurance—and then I'd be at work flying through the ledgers, and then back home taking Barnaby on his third walk of the day... it was just a total whirlwind. Everything was moving so fast, and I was moving so fast, that I wasn't really keeping track of the calendar. I was just living in this constant state of \"go, go, go.\"\n\nSo, if you need a specific number of days... I honestly couldn't tell you. I wish I could! I'm usually such a numbers person, obviously, being a bookkeeper and all. I like to have exact data for everything. But for that period? I really have no idea. It's all just this big, blurry, high-energy block of time in my memory. I didn't think to track it on a calendar because, well, I just thought it was my new normal. I thought I had finally just become this super-efficient, highly motivated adult. So yeah, I really don't have an exact day count for you, unfortunately. I just know it was a continuous stretch until I eventually crashed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Well, it's complicated, because at the time, I would have told you absolutely not. I thought I was employee of the month and the most put-together person on the planet! But looking back at the fallout... yeah, it definitely caused some issues. 

I mean, I’m still getting to work every day. I make sure I'm at my desk with my computer on by 8:30 sharp, so I haven't been fired or completely dropped the ball on my livelihood or anything extreme like that. But... remember how I said I was flying through all those expense reports and ledgers because I felt so energized? It turns out when you're moving at light speed, you aren't exactly being careful. I actually made enough reconciliation mistakes—like, really stupid things, transposing numbers on important vendor accounts, categorizing things in the wrong quarter—that my supervisor finally pulled me aside into his office to talk to me about it. 

It was honestly so mortifying. I am usually the one who finds the typos! I pride myself on being meticulous. He was pretty nice about it, but I could tell he was confused and concerned, and it just made me feel so incredibly embarrassed. Sitting in that little glass office while he pointed out my errors... I just wanted the floor to swallow me whole. 

And then outside of work, my relationships and my social life have just taken a massive hit. I've been completely avoiding my Thursday night salsa class. I've been going to that class for over a year, I even bought the proper suede-bottom shoes and I usually love it, but I just can't bring myself to go. And my friends keep texting me to get drinks or come over, and I've just been dodging them. I leave them on read, or I make up some lame excuse about unpacking boxes at the condo. I'm just avoiding everyone right now because I really don't feel like myself. First I was this hyper, snappy, intense person who was biting people's heads off, and now I'm just this deflated shell, and I don't want my friends or the people at my dance class to see me like this. I don't even know how to explain it to them. So, yeah, I guess it really has messed things up for me, even if I didn't see it while I was riding that high.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi, yeah, it's nice to meet you too. Thanks for seeing me. Um, well, what brings me in is that my sleep has just gotten really, really messed up lately. I mean, it's completely out of whack. I work as a bookkeeping clerk, so I'm staring at spreadsheets and numbers all day, and I really need to be focused, you know? If you miss a decimal point, it messes up the whole account. But I just can't seem to get any normal rest anymore to actually do my job right. \n\nI'll get into bed, try to do everything right, maybe read a little, but then I’ll either just stay up half the night with my mind completely running—just going over every little thing, thinking about work or just completely random thoughts that won't stop—and I'll look at the clock and it's 2 or 3 in the morning and I'm still wide awake. Or, on the flip side, if I do manage to fall asleep at a decent hour, I end up waking up way, way too early. Like, it's pitch black outside, maybe 4 AM, and I just lie there staring at the ceiling feeling like I never even rested at all. It's so incredibly frustrating because I know I need the sleep, and I just lie there trying to force it, but my brain just won't cooperate. I've tried turning off my phone early, I've tried tea, but nothing is working and it's just... it's exhausting, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different things, even when there isn’t an immediate problem to solve?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't really call it worrying, exactly. I mean, sure, I think about work, like I mentioned, because if I mess up a spreadsheet or an account reconciliation, it's a huge deal for my boss. But it's not like I'm lying there agonizing over... I don't know, my health, or the economy, or what someone said to me three weeks ago. It's not this constant dread or panic about things going wrong. \n\nIt's more just... my brain is just on. It's like a TV left on in the background that you can't find the remote for. And it's not always negative or anxious worry, either. Sometimes I'm just thinking about a movie I watched, or I'm planning out how I'm going to reorganize my apartment, or just completely random stuff. Like the other night, I think it was Tuesday, I literally spent two hours in the dark mentally redesigning a kitchen in a house I don't even own—I rent my place, so I can't even paint the cabinets if I wanted to! \n\nSo, yeah, it's not really worrying about problems that don't exist. People always ask me if I'm just stressed out, and I always have to explain that it's not really stress, it's just... noise. Lots and lots of noise and thoughts going a mile a minute, but not necessarily worrying. I just can't get it to power down so I can actually sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I really have. Honestly, it's been pretty bad. It's hard to even put into words because it's not just feeling a little sad about something specific, it's this... this incredibly heavy, empty feeling. Lately, I'll wake up—or, you know, when I'm just lying awake in the early morning like I was saying before—and that weight just hits me right in the chest before I even get out of bed. \n\nAnd the thing that really gets me, the thing that makes it so confusing and frustrating, is that I'll just be sitting there in my own condo, and I worked so, so hard for this place. I mean, I only have my high school diploma, so working my way up in bookkeeping and saving every single extra penny to finally get my own place was a massive deal for me. It took years of being super careful with my money. I should be so proud of it. I should be looking around at my space and feeling great about what I've accomplished. \n\nBut instead, I just sit there and I just want to cry. I'll literally just be sitting on my couch in this place I busted my tail to buy, and tears will just start welling up, and everything just feels so completely hollow and pointless. It doesn't make any sense, but I just can't shake that heavy sadness. It just hangs over me, making the whole day feel like I'm walking through wet cement, even when I'm just at home where I should be happy.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I really have. And honestly, that's one of the things that bothers me the most right now, because I've always been someone who had hobbies, you know? Like, to balance out the boring spreadsheet stuff at work, I always tried to make sure I was doing fun things for myself. But lately... I don't know. \n\nTake my salsa class, for example. I used to go every single Thursday night. I even bought these specific shoes for it, and I loved the music and just getting to move around after sitting at a desk all week. But I just... I completely stopped going. I think I missed one week, and then another, and now the thought of actually getting dressed and going down to the dance studio just feels entirely pointless. I can't muster up a single ounce of care for it, which is so unlike me. \n\nAnd it's not just going out, it's stuff at home, too. I've always really liked painting. Just watercolors, nothing professional, but it was my way to unwind in the evenings. I actually have my paints and my brushes and everything still sitting out on my dining table right now. I left them out weeks ago thinking, 'Oh, I'll definitely paint this weekend, I just need to leave it out so I remember.' But they've just been sitting there gathering dust. \n\nI walk past that table every single day, and I look at my paints, and I just feel... nothing. Absolutely nothing. It's the weirdest, saddest sensation. It's honestly like they belong to somebody else. Like I'm looking at a stranger's hobbies in a stranger's apartment. I used to lose hours just mixing colors and now I can't even bring myself to pick up a brush, and I just don't understand where the girl who loved doing those things went. Everything just feels so incredibly flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: No... no, I haven't. I mean, I know I just said everything feels completely pointless, and it does, it really does feel that way. But actually wanting to hurt myself? No, I would never do that. It's more like... I just want to pause everything, you know? \n\nI was actually thinking about this just the other day when I was driving to the grocery store. I was sitting at this red light on 4th street—it's this notoriously long light, you can sit there for like three whole minutes—and I was just feeling so incredibly heavy, like I was telling you before. And I just stared at the steering wheel and I realized that I don't want to die, I just really, really don't want to have to deal with today. Or tomorrow, for that matter. \n\nI would never actually take steps to end my life or anything like that. For one thing, I think about my family, and even just my cat, Barnaby. Like, who would feed Barnaby? He has a very specific wet food diet because of his stomach issues, and I couldn't just leave him. And like I said about my condo, I worked way too hard balancing books and saving every single penny to just throw it all away. I don't want to lose what I've built, even if I can't seem to enjoy it right now. \n\nSo, no, the thought is never that I want to end it all. It's more just this desperate wish that I could go to sleep and wake up when this whole heavy, empty phase is just over. Like hibernation, almost! If I could just hibernate like a bear for a month or two until I feel like myself again, that would be absolutely perfect. But no, no actual thoughts of hurting myself. I definitely couldn't do that.\n\nDoctor: For about how long have you been feeling this depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Well... I'd say it's been a while now. This whole heavy, depressed, guilty fog... it's been hanging around for about five months, I think. Yeah, right around five months. It's funny, because it really started creeping in back in the late winter, just when everything was supposed to be coming together. \n\nI mean, I remember the exact week because it was right when I was finalizing the closing on the condo. I had all these meetings with the mortgage broker, and I was signing what felt like a million pieces of paper. And you'd think I'd be ecstatic, right? Like, I'm 26, I only have a high school diploma, and I'm buying property. I should have been over the moon. But instead, I just started feeling this... this creeping sense of dread and sadness. \n\nAnd then, after I actually bought the place and moved in, it just got so much worse. The first couple of weeks were okay, I guess, just because I was so busy unpacking boxes and figuring out where to put my couch and everything. But once the dust settled and it was just me and Barnaby in this new place... the bottom just completely fell out. That's when the really deep emptiness started. So, it's been about five months total of feeling like this, but definitely much, much worse over the last couple of months since the move. It's just so frustrating because this was supposed to be my big, happy milestone, you know? And instead, it's just been this endless, dragging weight for almost half a year now.\n\nDoctor: Did your sleep problems start before you began feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, no, actually, the sleep stuff is pretty much what finally made me make an appointment. I mean, the depression has been there for five months, like I said, but the sleep problems... they really started acting up more recently, and that's honestly what brings me in today. It's just gotten so incredibly messed up, and I can't function like this anymore. \n\nIt's like... it's like my brain forgot how to sleep properly. Half the time, I'll go to bed, you know, do my whole routine—wash my face, brush my teeth, make sure Barnaby has his water—and I'll get under the covers, and my mind just starts running. And I don't even know what it's running about! It's just racing, and I'll end up staring at the ceiling, staying up half the night just tossing and turning. I'll look at the clock and it's 2 AM, then 3 AM, and I'm just lying there. \n\nAnd then, if I do manage to fall asleep, or on the nights where I don't have the racing thoughts at the beginning, I end up waking up way, way too early. Like, I'll jolt awake at 4:30 in the morning, and the alarm isn't set until 7:00, and I just feel completely exhausted. Like I never even rested at all. My body feels heavy, my eyes burn, but I can't get back to sleep. So I just lie there in the dark, in this new condo that I'm supposed to be so happy about, just feeling miserable and exhausted. It's so frustrating because I'm an accountant, well, a bookkeeping clerk, and I need my brain to be sharp for the spreadsheets and the numbers, but I'm just running on fumes. So yeah, the sleep getting this bad is really the main reason I'm sitting here talking to you right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking noticeably more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Well, it's funny you ask that, because I've actually noticed both, and it's driving me completely crazy. Like, at work... I'm a bookkeeping clerk, right? So my whole day is numbers and spreadsheets and calculators. And lately, there are times when I'm just sitting there at my desk, and I literally catch myself moving in slow motion. Like, I'll be holding a stack of invoices, and I'll just stare at the calculator for what feels like forever before I even press the first button. It's like my brain has to send a special, certified letter to my hand just to get it to move. My boss actually asked me the other day if I was feeling okay because it took me twice as long to finish the end-of-day reconciliation. I just felt so heavy and sluggish, like I was moving through molasses. \n\nBut then... then there are other times where it's the exact opposite! It's so weird. Like, I'll be sitting there, and suddenly I just can't sit still. My leg will start bouncing under the desk, and I'll be fidgeting with all the little loose receipts on my desk, folding them up into tiny squares or rolling them into little tubes. I'll pick up a pen and click it a hundred times until my coworker gives me a look, and then I'll realize I'm doing it and try to stop, but I just feel this... this restless energy, like I have to be moving something. \n\nSo it's like I'm either a complete zombie who can barely lift a finger to type in a number, or I'm this jittery mess who can't stop playing with the office supplies. There's really no in-between right now. And it's so frustrating because I used to be so efficient! I could fly through a stack of expense reports in no time, and now it's just this constant battle of either forcing myself to move or trying to force myself to sit still. It's exhausting, honestly. Just another thing that's completely out of whack lately.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you ever felt you needed much less sleep than usual and still had plenty of energy the next day?\n\nPatient: Oh, yeah... actually, yes. It's so strange to think about now because of how completely drained I feel lately, but before I crashed into this awful slump, there was this stretch of time where that exact thing happened. It was right around when I was doing all the initial condo paperwork, I think. \n\nI just remember there were several nights where I would only sleep maybe three or four hours, tops. I'd be up until 2 or 3 in the morning because my brain was just buzzing with ideas—I was looking up living room furniture online, re-doing all my personal budget spreadsheets to account for the new mortgage, organizing literally everything. And then I'd wake up at like 6 AM, and the crazy part is, I wasn't tired at all. I got up feeling completely charged. \n\nUsually, I am the kind of person who needs a massive travel mug of dark roast just to function and look at the numbers at work, but during that time? It was like coffee wasn't even necessary. I would just bounce right out of bed, take Barnaby for a long walk, maybe even do a load of laundry or scrub the baseboards before I even had to leave for the office. I honestly thought I was just finally hitting my stride, you know? Like, I thought, \"Wow, this is what it feels like to be a successful, motivated adult. I'm 26, I'm buying a place, I'm crushing it at the bookkeeping firm.\" I was flying through expense reports so fast my boss was actually impressed. I just felt like I had this unlimited battery pack. But then, of course, it just completely vanished, and now I'm stuck feeling like a zombie who can't sleep properly anyway. But yeah, for a little while there, three or four hours was all I needed and I felt completely unstoppable.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you only slept a few hours and still felt energized, did you also feel unusually upbeat or unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, it was a really bizarre mix of both, which I know sounds totally contradictory, but that's exactly how it was. Like, at first, and for a lot of that time, I felt incredible. Like I was just... lit up from the inside. I felt so upbeat and positive, like everything was finally falling into place. I was getting the condo, I was organizing my life, I felt completely unstoppable. I remember walking into the office and just beaming at everyone, feeling like I could take on the world and balance every single ledger in the building before lunch. It was this amazing high, like I was the best version of myself. \n\nBut then... the weird part is, it could just flip on a dime. Like, out of nowhere, I would get so incredibly irritable. And not just a little annoyed, but like, skin-crawlingly frustrated over the absolute smallest things. I remember one day my mom called me to ask a super basic question about the closing costs for the condo—something she was just trying to be helpful about, you know?—and I completely snapped at her. I practically bit her head off on the phone, telling her she was hovering and treating me like a child, which was so unfair of me. And it wasn't just her. At work, my supervisor came over to ask a really standard question about how I coded one of the vendor invoices—something he does all the time, totally normal—and I felt this rush of pure anger. I had to physically bite the inside of my cheek to stop myself from yelling at him that he was slowing me down. I just gave him this incredibly short, snippy answer and glared at him until he walked away. \n\nNormally, I am not like that at all! I'm usually the one who brings donuts on Fridays and helps people find the typos in their spreadsheets. I don't snap at people. But during those few days, it was like I was this unstoppable force, and if anyone dared to interrupt my flow or ask a question, I just couldn't handle it. It was like they were moving in slow motion and I was moving at light speed, and their slowness was just infuriating to me. So yeah, I was super upbeat, but also ready to bite someone's head off if they looked at me wrong. It was exhausting for everyone around me, I'm sure.\n\nDoctor: About how many days in a row did that energized period last?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... how many days in a row? You know, I've actually tried to figure that out myself, looking back, but... well, it's incredibly hard to pin down an exact number. Because when you're only sleeping three or four hours a night, the days just sort of... they bleed into one another, you know? It's like one long, continuous stretch of daytime. You don't get that normal reset of a full eight hours, so Tuesday just rolls right into Wednesday, and suddenly it's Friday and you're still running on this bizarre high. \n\nI just remember it was right in the thick of all that condo stuff. I'd be up at 2 AM sending emails to my realtor—who probably thought I was completely insane, by the way, getting emails at that hour about escrow and title insurance—and then I'd be at work flying through the ledgers, and then back home taking Barnaby on his third walk of the day... it was just a total whirlwind. Everything was moving so fast, and I was moving so fast, that I wasn't really keeping track of the calendar. I was just living in this constant state of \"go, go, go.\"\n\nSo, if you need a specific number of days... I honestly couldn't tell you. I wish I could! I thought I had finally just become this super-efficient, highly motivated adult. So yeah, I really don't have an exact day count for you, unfortunately. I just know it was a continuous stretch until I eventually crashed.\n\nDoctor: During that energized period, did it cause serious problems for you at work, financially, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, it's complicated, because at the time, I would have told you absolutely not. I actually made enough reconciliation mistakes—like, really stupid things, transposing numbers on important vendor accounts, categorizing things in the wrong quarter—that my supervisor finally pulled me aside into his office to talk to me about it. \n\nIt was honestly so mortifying. He was pretty nice about it, but I could tell he was confused and concerned, and it just made me feel so incredibly embarrassed. Sitting in that little glass office while he pointed out my errors... I just wanted the floor to swallow me whole. \n\nAnd then outside of work, my relationships and my social life have just taken a massive hit. I've been completely avoiding my Thursday night salsa class. I've been going to that class for over a year, I even bought the proper suede-bottom shoes and I usually love it, but I just can't bring myself to go. And my friends keep texting me to get drinks or come over, and I've just been dodging them. I leave them on read, or I make up some lame excuse about unpacking boxes at the condo. I'm just avoiding everyone right now because I really don't feel like myself. First I was this hyper, snappy, intense person who was biting people's heads off, and now I'm just this deflated shell, and I don't want my friends or the people at my dance class to see me like this. I don't even know how to explain it to them. So, yeah, I guess it really has messed things up for me, even if I didn't see it while I was riding that high.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. And not just a little annoyed, but like, skin-crawlingly frustrated over the absolute smallest things. I remember one day my mom called me to ask a super basic question about the closing costs for the condo—something she was just trying to be helpful about, you know?—and I completely snapped at her. I practically bit her head off on the phone, telling her she was hovering and treating me like a child, which was so unfair of me. And it wasn't just her. At work, my supervisor came over to ask a really standard question about how I coded one of the vendor invoices—something he does all the time, totally normal—and I felt this rush of pure anger. I had to physically bite the inside of my cheek to stop myself from yelling at him that he was slowing me down. I just gave him this incredibly short, snippy answer and glared at him until he walked away. \n\nNormally, I am not like that at all! I'm usually the one who brings donuts on Fridays and helps people find the typos in their spreadsheets. I don't snap at people. But during those few days, it was like I was this unstoppable force, and if anyone dared to interrupt my flow or ask a question, I just couldn't handle it. It was like they were moving in slow motion and I was moving at light speed, and their slowness was just infuriating to me. So yeah, I was super upbeat, but also ready to bite someone's head off if they looked at me wrong. It was exhausting for everyone around me, I'm sure.\n\nDoctor: About how many days in a row did that energized period last?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... how many days in a row? You know, I've actually tried to figure that out myself, looking back, but... well, it's incredibly hard to pin down an exact number. Because when you're only sleeping three or four hours a night, the days just sort of... they bleed into one another, you know? It's like one long, continuous stretch of daytime. You don't get that normal reset of a full eight hours, so Tuesday just rolls right into Wednesday, and suddenly it's Friday and you're still running on this bizarre high. \n\nI just remember it was right in the thick of all that condo stuff. I'd be up at 2 AM sending emails to my realtor—who probably thought I was completely insane, by the way, getting emails at that hour about escrow and title insurance—and then I'd be at work flying through the ledgers, and then back home taking Barnaby on his third walk of the day... it was just a total whirlwind. Everything was moving so fast, and I was moving so fast, that I wasn't really keeping track of the calendar. I was just living in this constant state of \"go, go, go.\"\n\nSo, if you need a specific number of days... I honestly couldn't tell you. I wish I could! I'm usually such a numbers person, obviously, being a bookkeeper and all. I like to have exact data for everything. But for that period? I really have no idea. It's all just this big, blurry, high-energy block of time in my memory. I didn't think to track it on a calendar because, well, I just thought it was my new normal. I thought I had finally just become this super-efficient, highly motivated adult. So yeah, I really don't have an exact day count for you, unfortunately. I just know it was a continuous stretch until I eventually crashed.\n\nDoctor: During that energized period, did it cause serious problems for you at work, financially, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, it's complicated, because at the time, I would have told you absolutely not. I thought I was employee of the month and the most put-together person on the planet! But looking back at the fallout... yeah, it definitely caused some issues. \n\nI mean, I’m still getting to work every day. I make sure I'm at my desk with my computer on by 8:30 sharp, so I haven't been fired or completely dropped the ball on my livelihood or anything extreme like that. But... remember how I said I was flying through all those expense reports and ledgers because I felt so energized? It turns out when you're moving at light speed, you aren't exactly being careful. I actually made enough reconciliation mistakes—like, really stupid things, transposing numbers on important vendor accounts, categorizing things in the wrong quarter—that my supervisor finally pulled me aside into his office to talk to me about it. \n\nIt was honestly so mortifying. I am usually the one who finds the typos! I pride myself on being meticulous. He was pretty nice about it, but I could tell he was confused and concerned, and it just made me feel so incredibly embarrassed. Sitting in that little glass office while he pointed out my errors... I just wanted the floor to swallow me whole. \n\nAnd then outside of work, my relationships and my social life have just taken a massive hit. I've been completely avoiding my Thursday night salsa class. I've been going to that class for over a year, I even bought the proper suede-bottom shoes and I usually love it, but I just can't bring myself to go. And my friends keep texting me to get drinks or come over, and I've just been dodging them. I leave them on read, or I make up some lame excuse about unpacking boxes at the condo. I'm just avoiding everyone right now because I really don't feel like myself. First I was this hyper, snappy, intense person who was biting people's heads off, and now I'm just this deflated shell, and I don't want my friends or the people at my dance class to see me like this. I don't even know how to explain it to them. So, yeah, I guess it really has messed things up for me, even if I didn't see it while I was riding that high.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know you're trying to get a specific number, like four days, but I just... I really can't say for sure. I mean, it could have been four days? It could have been three, or maybe five? It's just so frustrating because usually, if you asked me how long something took, I could pull up a spreadsheet in my brain and tell you exactly. Like, closing on the condo took exactly thirty-two days from the offer being accepted. I know that for a fact. 

But this? When you aren't really sleeping, the concept of a "day" just completely breaks down. Is a day twenty-four hours? Is a day the time between when the sun comes up and goes down? Because I was awake for all of it. I'd be watching the sunrise with Barnaby and thinking, "Okay, is this a new day, or just a continuation of yesterday?" So when you ask if it was at least four days in a row, I just keep looping back to that feeling of one giant, endless stretch of time. 

I really wish I had kept a journal or something. I actually bought this really nice leather-bound planner from Target right before all this started, thinking I was going to be super organized for the move, color-coding my packing boxes and everything. If I had just used it to track my sleep instead of just my escrow appointments, I could give you a straight answer right now. But I didn't. So I really, honestly don't know if it hit that four-day mark. I'm sorry, I just know it was... a while.

I mean, it's completely out of whack. I work as a bookkeeping clerk, so I'm staring at spreadsheets and numbers all day, and I really need to be focused, you know? If you miss a decimal point, it messes up the whole account. But I just can't seem to get any normal rest anymore to actually do my job right. \n\nI'll get into bed, try to do everything right, maybe read a little, but then I’ll either just stay up half the night with my mind completely running—just going over every little thing, thinking about work or just completely random thoughts that won't stop—and I'll look at the clock and it's 2 or 3 in the morning and I'm still wide awake. Like, it's pitch black outside, maybe 4 AM, and I just lie there staring at the ceiling feeling like I never even rested at all. It doesn't make any sense, but I just can't shake that heavy sadness. It just hangs over me, making the whole day feel like I'm walking through wet cement, even when I'm just at home where I should be happy.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I really have. And honestly, that's one of the things that bothers me the most right now, because I've always been someone who had hobbies, you know? Like, to balance out the boring spreadsheet stuff at work, I always tried to make sure I was doing fun things for myself. But lately... I don't know. \n\nTake my salsa class, for example. I used to go every single Thursday night. I even bought these specific shoes for it, and I loved the music and just getting to move around after sitting at a desk all week. But I just... I completely stopped going. I think I missed one week, and then another, and now the thought of actually getting dressed and going down to the dance studio just feels entirely pointless. I can't muster up a single ounce of care for it, which is so unlike me. \n\nAnd it's not just going out, it's stuff at home, too. I've always really liked painting. Just watercolors, nothing professional, but it was my way to unwind in the evenings. I actually have my paints and my brushes and everything still sitting out on my dining table right now. I left them out weeks ago thinking, 'Oh, I'll definitely paint this weekend, I just need to leave it out so I remember.' But they've just been sitting there gathering dust. \n\nI walk past that table every single day, and I look at my paints, and I just feel... nothing. Absolutely nothing. It's the weirdest, saddest sensation. It's honestly like they belong to somebody else. Like I'm looking at a stranger's hobbies in a stranger's apartment. I used to lose hours just mixing colors and now I can't even bring myself to pick up a brush, and I just don't understand where the girl who loved doing those things went. Everything just feels so incredibly flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: No... no, I haven't. I mean, I know I just said everything feels completely pointless, and it does, it really does feel that way. But actually wanting to hurt myself? No, I would never do that. It's more like... I just want to pause everything, you know? \n\nI was actually thinking about this just the other day when I was driving to the grocery store. I was sitting at this red light on 4th street—it's this notoriously long light, you can sit there for like three whole minutes—and I was just feeling so incredibly heavy, like I was telling you before. And like I said about my condo, I worked way too hard balancing books and saving every single penny to just throw it all away. I don't want to lose what I've built, even if I can't seem to enjoy it right now. \n\nSo, no, the thought is never that I want to end it all. It's more just this desperate wish that I could go to sleep and wake up when this whole heavy, empty phase is just over. Like hibernation, almost! If I could just hibernate like a bear for a month or two until I feel like myself again, that would be absolutely perfect. But no, no actual thoughts of hurting myself. I definitely couldn't do that.\n\nDoctor: For about how long have you been feeling this depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Well... I'd say it's been a while now. This whole heavy, depressed, guilty fog... it's been hanging around for about five months, I think. Yeah, right around five months. I had all these meetings with the mortgage broker, and I was signing what felt like a million pieces of paper. And you'd think I'd be ecstatic, right? But instead, I just started feeling this... this creeping sense of dread and sadness. \n\nAnd then, after I actually bought the place and moved in, it just got so much worse. It's just so frustrating because this was supposed to be my big, happy milestone, you know? And instead, it's just been this endless, dragging weight for almost half a year now.\n\nDoctor: Did your sleep problems start before you began feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, no, actually, the sleep stuff is pretty much what finally made me make an appointment. It's just gotten so incredibly messed up, and I can't function like this anymore. \n\nIt's like... it's like my brain forgot how to sleep properly. Half the time, I'll go to bed, you know, do my whole routine—wash my face, brush my teeth, make sure Barnaby has his water—and I'll get under the covers, and my mind just starts running. And I don't even know what it's running about! It's just racing, and I'll end up staring at the ceiling, staying up half the night just tossing and turning. I'll look at the clock and it's 2 AM, then 3 AM, and I'm just lying there. \n\nAnd then, if I do manage to fall asleep, or on the nights where I don't have the racing thoughts at the beginning, I end up waking up way, way too early. Like, I'll jolt awake at 4:30 in the morning, and the alarm isn't set until 7:00, and I just feel completely exhausted. Like I never even rested at all. My body feels heavy, my eyes burn, but I can't get back to sleep. So I just lie there in the dark, in this new condo that I'm supposed to be so happy about, just feeling miserable and exhausted. It's so frustrating because I'm an accountant, well, a bookkeeping clerk, and I need my brain to be sharp for the spreadsheets and the numbers, but I'm just running on fumes. So yeah, the sleep getting this bad is really the main reason I'm sitting here talking to you right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking noticeably more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Well, it's funny you ask that, because I've actually noticed both, and it's driving me completely crazy. It's like my brain has to send a special, certified letter to my hand just to get it to move. I just felt so heavy and sluggish, like I was moving through molasses. \n\nBut then... then there are other times where it's the exact opposite! It's so weird. Like, I'll be sitting there, and suddenly I just can't sit still. My leg will start bouncing under the desk, and I'll be fidgeting with all the little loose receipts on my desk, folding them up into tiny squares or rolling them into little tubes. I'll pick up a pen and click it a hundred times until my coworker gives me a look, and then I'll realize I'm doing it and try to stop, but I just feel this... this restless energy, like I have to be moving something. \n\nSo it's like I'm either a complete zombie who can barely lift a finger to type in a number, or I'm this jittery mess who can't stop playing with the office supplies. There's really no in-between right now. And it's so frustrating because I used to be so efficient! I could fly through a stack of expense reports in no time, and now it's just this constant battle of either forcing myself to move or trying to force myself to sit still. It's exhausting, honestly. Just another thing that's completely out of whack lately.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you ever felt you needed much less sleep than usual and still had plenty of energy the next day?\n\nPatient: Oh, yeah... actually, yes. It's so strange to think about now because of how completely drained I feel lately, but before I crashed into this awful slump, there was this stretch of time where that exact thing happened. It was right around when I was doing all the initial condo paperwork, I think. \n\nI just remember there were several nights where I would only sleep maybe three or four hours, tops. I'd be up until 2 or 3 in the morning because my brain was just buzzing with ideas—I was looking up living room furniture online, re-doing all my personal budget spreadsheets to account for the new mortgage, organizing literally everything. And then I'd wake up at like 6 AM, and the crazy part is, I wasn't tired at all. I got up feeling completely charged. \n\nUsually, I am the kind of person who needs a massive travel mug of dark roast just to function and look at the numbers at work, but during that time? It was like coffee wasn't even necessary. I would just bounce right out of bed, take Barnaby for a long walk, maybe even do a load of laundry or scrub the baseboards before I even had to leave for the office. I honestly thought I was just finally hitting my stride, you know? Like, I thought, \"Wow, this is what it feels like to be a successful, motivated adult. I'm 26, I'm buying a place, I'm crushing it at the bookkeeping firm.\" I was flying through expense reports so fast my boss was actually impressed. I just felt like I had this unlimited battery pack. But then, of course, it just completely vanished, and now I'm stuck feeling like a zombie who can't sleep properly anyway. But yeah, for a little while there, three or four hours was all I needed and I felt completely unstoppable.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you only slept a few hours and still felt energized, did you also feel unusually upbeat or unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, it was a really bizarre mix of both, which I know sounds totally contradictory, but that's exactly how it was. Like, at first, and for a lot of that time, I felt incredible. Like I was just... lit up from the inside. I felt so upbeat and positive, like everything was finally falling into place. I was getting the condo, I was organizing my life, I felt completely unstoppable. I remember walking into the office and just beaming at everyone, feeling like I could take on the world and balance every single ledger in the building before lunch. It was this amazing high, like I was the best version of myself. \n\nBut then... the weird part is, it could just flip on a dime. Like, out of nowhere, I would get so incredibly irritable. And not just a little annoyed, but like, skin-crawlingly frustrated over the absolute smallest things. I remember one day my mom called me to ask a super basic question about the closing costs for the condo—something she was just trying to be helpful about, you know?—and I completely snapped at her. I practically bit her head off on the phone, telling her she was hovering and treating me like a child, which was so unfair of me. And it wasn't just her. At work, my supervisor came over to ask a really standard question about how I coded one of the vendor invoices—something he does all the time, totally normal—and I felt this rush of pure anger. I had to physically bite the inside of my cheek to stop myself from yelling at him that he was slowing me down. I just gave him this incredibly short, snippy answer and glared at him until he walked away. \n\nNormally, I am not like that at all! I'm usually the one who brings donuts on Fridays and helps people find the typos in their spreadsheets. I don't snap at people. But during those few days, it was like I was this unstoppable force, and if anyone dared to interrupt my flow or ask a question, I just couldn't handle it. It was like they were moving in slow motion and I was moving at light speed, and their slowness was just infuriating to me. So yeah, I was super upbeat, but also ready to bite someone's head off if they looked at me wrong. It was exhausting for everyone around me, I'm sure.\n\nDoctor: About how many days in a row did that energized period last?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... how many days in a row? You know, I've actually tried to figure that out myself, looking back, but... well, it's incredibly hard to pin down an exact number. Because when you're only sleeping three or four hours a night, the days just sort of... they bleed into one another, you know? It's like one long, continuous stretch of daytime. You don't get that normal reset of a full eight hours, so Tuesday just rolls right into Wednesday, and suddenly it's Friday and you're still running on this bizarre high. \n\nI just remember it was right in the thick of all that condo stuff. I'd be up at 2 AM sending emails to my realtor—who probably thought I was completely insane, by the way, getting emails at that hour about escrow and title insurance—and then I'd be at work flying through the ledgers, and then back home taking Barnaby on his third walk of the day... it was just a total whirlwind. Everything was moving so fast, and I was moving so fast, that I wasn't really keeping track of the calendar. I was just living in this constant state of \"go, go, go.\"\n\nSo, if you need a specific number of days... I honestly couldn't tell you. I wish I could! I'm usually such a numbers person, obviously, being a bookkeeper and all. I like to have exact data for everything. But for that period? I really have no idea. It's all just this big, blurry, high-energy block of time in my memory. I didn't think to track it on a calendar because, well, I just thought it was my new normal. I thought I had finally just become this super-efficient, highly motivated adult. So yeah, I really don't have an exact day count for you, unfortunately. I just know it was a continuous stretch until I eventually crashed.\n\nDoctor: During that energized period, did it cause serious problems for you at work, financially, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, it's complicated, because at the time, I would have told you absolutely not. I thought I was employee of the month and the most put-together person on the planet! But looking back at the fallout... yeah, it definitely caused some issues. \n\nI mean, I’m still getting to work every day. I make sure I'm at my desk with my computer on by 8:30 sharp, so I haven't been fired or completely dropped the ball on my livelihood or anything extreme like that. But... remember how I said I was flying through all those expense reports and ledgers because I felt so energized? It turns out when you're moving at light speed, you aren't exactly being careful. I actually made enough reconciliation mistakes—like, really stupid things, transposing numbers on important vendor accounts, categorizing things in the wrong quarter—that my supervisor finally pulled me aside into his office to talk to me about it. \n\nIt was honestly so mortifying. I am usually the one who finds the typos! I pride myself on being meticulous. He was pretty nice about it, but I could tell he was confused and concerned, and it just made me feel so incredibly embarrassed. Sitting in that little glass office while he pointed out my errors... I just wanted the floor to swallow me whole. \n\nAnd then outside of work, my relationships and my social life have just taken a massive hit. I've been completely avoiding my Thursday night salsa class. I've been going to that class for over a year, I even bought the proper suede-bottom shoes and I usually love it, but I just can't bring myself to go. And my friends keep texting me to get drinks or come over, and I've just been dodging them. I leave them on read, or I make up some lame excuse about unpacking boxes at the condo. I'm just avoiding everyone right now because I really don't feel like myself. First I was this hyper, snappy, intense person who was biting people's heads off, and now I'm just this deflated shell, and I don't want my friends or the people at my dance class to see me like this. I don't even know how to explain it to them. So, yeah, I guess it really has messed things up for me, even if I didn't see it while I was riding that high.\n\nDoctor: Did that energized, low-sleep period last at least 4 days in a row?\n\nPatient: I know you're trying to get a specific number, like four days, but I just... I really can't say for sure. I mean, it could have been four days? It could have been three, or maybe five? It's just so frustrating because usually, if you asked me how long something took, I could pull up a spreadsheet in my brain and tell you exactly. Like, closing on the condo took exactly thirty-two days from the offer being accepted. I know that for a fact. \n\nBut this? When you aren't really sleeping, the concept of a \"day\" just completely breaks down. Is a day twenty-four hours? Is a day the time between when the sun comes up and goes down? Because I was awake for all of it. I'd be watching the sunrise with Barnaby and thinking, \"Okay, is this a new day, or just a continuation of yesterday?\" So when you ask if it was at least four days in a row, I just keep looping back to that feeling of one giant, endless stretch of time. \n\nI really wish I had kept a journal or something. I actually bought this really nice leather-bound planner from Target right before all this started, thinking I was going to be super organized for the move, color-coding my packing boxes and everything. If I had just used it to track my sleep instead of just my escrow appointments, I could give you a straight answer right now. But I didn't. So I really, honestly don't know if it hit that four-day mark. I'm sorry, I just know it was... a while.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
